- **Authors**: Farzan Moodi, Fereshteh Khodadadi Shoushtari, Gelareh Valizadeh, Dornaz Mazinani, Hanieh Mobarak Salari, Hamidreza Saligheh Rad
- **Abstract**: Accurate segmentation of glioma brain tumors is crucial for diagnosis and treatment planning. Deep learning techniques offer promising solutions, but optimal model architectures remain under investigation. We used the BraTS 2021 dataset, selecting T1 with contrast enhancement (T1CE), T2, and Fluid-Attenuated Inversion Recovery (FLAIR) sequences for model development. The proposed Attention Xception UNet (AXUNet) architecture integrates an Xception backbone with dot-product self-attention modules, inspired by state-of-the-art (SOTA) large language models such as Google Bard and OpenAI ChatGPT, within a UNet-shaped model. We compared AXUNet with SOTA models. Comparative evaluation on the test set demonstrated improved results over baseline models. Inception-UNet and Xception-UNet achieved mean Dice scores of 90.88 and 93.24, respectively. Attention ResUNet (AResUNet) attained a mean Dice score of 92.80, with the highest score of 84.92 for enhancing tumor (ET) among all models. Attention Gate UNet (AGUNet) yielded a mean Dice score of 90.38. AXUNet outperformed all models with a mean Dice score of 93.73. It demonstrated superior Dice scores across whole tumor (WT) and tumor core (TC) regions, achieving 92.59 for WT, 86.81 for TC, and 84.89 for ET. The integration of the Xception backbone and dot-product self-attention mechanisms in AXUNet showcases enhanced performance in capturing spatial and contextual information. The findings underscore the potential utility of AXUNet in facilitating precise tumor delineation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AXUNet (Attention Xception UNet), a novel deep learning architecture for brain tumor segmentation. It combines the UNet architecture with Xception blocks for efficient feature extraction and a self-attention mechanism inspired by large language models to focus on relevant tumor regions. The paper compares AXUNet to existing state-of-the-art models (UNet, Inception-UNet, ResUNet, Attention ResUNet, and Attention Gate UNet) on the BraTS 2021 dataset, demonstrating improved segmentation performance in terms of Dice scores, particularly for whole tumor (WT) segmentation. The paper also explores model interpretability using Grad-CAM visualization and discusses limitations and future research directions, including refining the self-attention module and focusing on more advanced MR imaging for edema region delineation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of Xception blocks with self-attention modules in a UNet architecture is a novel contribution. Drawing inspiration from the architecture of large language models for image segmentation is a worthwhile direction of exploration. The approach aims to enhance spatial and contextual information capture. However, the individual components (UNet, Xception, self-attention) are well-established, and the innovation primarily lies in their integration.
*   **Significance:** Accurate brain tumor segmentation is crucial for diagnosis and treatment planning, so any improvements in segmentation accuracy have potential clinical significance. The performance improvements shown in the paper, while present, are not dramatically superior to other methods like Attention ResUNet. It's also important to note that the self-attention mechanism introduces additional computational complexity. The paper highlights improved performance in WT segmentation, but the results are less conclusive for TC and ET. The Grad-CAM analysis provides some insight into model behavior, but further investigation is required to fully understand the impact of the attention module. The study acknowledges the computational demands of the self-attention mechanism and suggests further refinement, which is a realistic self-assessment.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-organized presentation.
    *   Comprehensive comparison with relevant baseline models.
    *   Ablation study to demonstrate the contribution of each module.
    *   Discussion of limitations and future research directions.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The improvement in Dice score compared to Attention ResUNet, while positive, is not large and may not justify the added complexity of the model.
    *   The computational cost is not thoroughly discussed and could be a limiting factor in clinical applications.
    *   Performance in ET and TC regions is not convincingly better than existing methods.
    *   The reliance on the BraTS dataset might limit generalizability due to inherent biases and variations in image acquisition protocols across different centers.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a novel architecture and demonstrates promising results for brain tumor segmentation. The integration of ideas from large language models into medical image segmentation is an innovative approach. However, the performance gains over existing state-of-the-art methods are modest, and the computational cost is a concern. The lack of a clear clinical benefit based on the segmentation improvements is also a limiting factor. While the work presents a valuable contribution and encourages further research into the use of self-attention mechanisms for medical imaging, the marginal improvements and unaddressed computational costs warrant a score in the middle range.

### Data-driven Seasonal Climate Predictions via Variational Inference and Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20466v1)
- **Authors**: Lluís Palma, Alejandro Peraza, David Civantos, Amanda Duarte, Stefano Materia, Ángel G. Muñoz, Jesús Peña, Laia Romero, Albert Soret, Markus G. Donat
- **Abstract**: Most operational climate services providers base their seasonal predictions on initialised general circulation models (GCMs) or statistical techniques that fit past observations. GCMs require substantial computational resources, which limits their capacity. In contrast, statistical methods often lack robustness due to short historical records. Recent works propose machine learning methods trained on climate model output, leveraging larger sample sizes and simulated scenarios. Yet, many of these studies focus on prediction tasks that might be restricted in spatial extent or temporal coverage, opening a gap with existing operational predictions. Thus, the present study evaluates the effectiveness of a methodology that combines variational inference with transformer models to predict fields of seasonal anomalies. The predictions cover all four seasons and are initialised one month before the start of each season. The model was trained on climate model output from CMIP6 and tested using ERA5 reanalysis data. We analyse the method's performance in predicting interannual anomalies beyond the climate change-induced trend. We also test the proposed methodology in a regional context with a use case focused on Europe. While climate change trends dominate the skill of temperature predictions, the method presents additional skill over the climatological forecast in regions influenced by known teleconnections. We reach similar conclusions based on the validation of precipitation predictions. Despite underperforming SEAS5 in most tropics, our model offers added value in numerous extratropical inland regions. This work demonstrates the effectiveness of training generative models on climate model output for seasonal predictions, providing skilful predictions beyond the induced climate change trend at time scales and lead times relevant for user applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a data-driven methodology for seasonal climate prediction using variational inference and vision transformers. The model is trained on climate model output from CMIP6 and validated against ERA5 reanalysis data, explicitly accounting for climate change-induced trends. The core idea is to combine variational inference with vision transformers to predict seasonal anomalies. The study analyzes the model's performance in predicting interannual anomalies beyond the climate change-induced trend, and it evaluates its effectiveness in a regional context (Europe) by varying spatial domains and resolutions. The results show skill in temperature predictions beyond trend-based forecasts and outperforming SEAS5 in some inland areas, although SEAS5 remains superior in equatorial regions. Precipitation forecasts demonstrate more limited skill. The generative capabilities enabled by variational autoencoders facilitate efficient ensemble generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The paper claims novelty in being the first application of vision transformers for seasonal prediction within a variational inference framework. While using machine learning for climate prediction is not new, the specific combination of vision transformers and variational autoencoders, along with the explicit handling of climate change trends, contributes to the methodological novelty. The evaluation across different spatial scales (global, regional at varying resolutions) also adds to this aspect. However, the individual components are established techniques, so the innovation lies in the specific integration and application.

*Significance:*

The paper demonstrates that this machine learning approach can produce skillful seasonal predictions, particularly for temperature, and even outperform a state-of-the-art dynamical system (SEAS5) in certain regions. This suggests potential for data-driven models to complement or enhance traditional climate forecasting methods. The findings regarding regional skill and the impact of varying resolution offer practical insights for developing tailored climate services.
However, the skill for precipitation prediction is weaker, indicating a limitation. The reliance on CMIP6 data for training also introduces a potential source of bias depending on how well those models capture the true climate variability, limiting its broader applicability.

*Strengths:*
*   **Clear Methodology:** The paper provides a well-defined explanation of the methodology.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Evaluation involves multiple metrics, comparison against a benchmark (SEAS5), and analysis at different spatial scales.
*   **Explicit Trend Handling:** The explicit treatment of climate change trends is a crucial and well-executed aspect.

*Weaknesses:*
*   **Limited Precipitation Skill:** The relatively poor performance for precipitation casts doubt on the general applicability of the approach.
*   **CMIP6 Reliance:** Dependence on CMIP6 introduces potential model biases. How the authors evaluated the model's sensitivity is not well addressed.
*   **Lack of Detailed Ablation Study:** A more detailed ablation study to understand the contributions of the individual components (ViT, CVAE, trend removal) could have strengthened the paper.
*   **Limited Discussion of Computational Cost:** While the paper mentions reduced computational cost for ensemble generation, a quantitative comparison with dynamical systems would be beneficial.

*Potential Impact:*

The paper could influence future research by encouraging further exploration of hybrid data-driven/dynamical approaches to seasonal climate prediction. It could inspire the development of regional climate services based on similar machine learning techniques. However, the limitations regarding precipitation and reliance on potentially biased climate model data may temper its overall impact.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel combination of existing techniques and demonstrates promising results for temperature prediction. However, the limitations in precipitation forecasting and the reliance on CMIP6 model output reduce its overall impact. A more in-depth ablation study and quantitative comparison with the computational cost of dynamical systems would have strengthened the paper significantly. Also, how well this approach captures extreme events is not addressed.

### From Trial to Triumph: Advancing Long Video Understanding via Visual Context Sample Scaling and Self-reward Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20472v1)
- **Authors**: Yucheng Suo, Fan Ma, Linchao Zhu, Tianyi Wang, Fengyun Rao, Yi Yang
- **Abstract**: Multi-modal Large language models (MLLMs) show remarkable ability in video understanding. Nevertheless, understanding long videos remains challenging as the models can only process a finite number of frames in a single inference, potentially omitting crucial visual information. To address the challenge, we propose generating multiple predictions through visual context sampling, followed by a scoring mechanism to select the final prediction. Specifically, we devise a bin-wise sampling strategy that enables MLLMs to generate diverse answers based on various combinations of keyframes, thereby enriching the visual context. To determine the final prediction from the sampled answers, we employ a self-reward by linearly combining three scores: (1) a frequency score indicating the prevalence of each option, (2) a marginal confidence score reflecting the inter-intra sample certainty of MLLM predictions, and (3) a reasoning score for different question types, including clue-guided answering for global questions and temporal self-refocusing for local questions. The frequency score ensures robustness through majority correctness, the confidence-aligned score reflects prediction certainty, and the typed-reasoning score addresses cases with sparse key visual information using tailored strategies. Experiments show that this approach covers the correct answer for a high percentage of long video questions, on seven datasets show that our method improves the performance of three MLLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "From Trial to Triumph: Advancing Long Video Understanding via Visual Context Sample Scaling and Self-reward Alignment" addresses the challenge of understanding long videos using Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs). The core idea is to improve performance by generating multiple predictions from different visual contexts within the video and then selecting the best prediction using a self-reward mechanism.  The approach involves a bin-wise sampling strategy for keyframe selection, followed by a self-reward score that combines frequency, marginal confidence, and reasoning scores tailored to different question types (global and local). The reasoning score utilizes external LLMs and temporal self-refocusing to address sparse key visual information. The paper demonstrates improvements on several long video understanding benchmarks using three MLLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies primarily in the combination of existing techniques (multiple sampling, self-reward) in a novel way specifically designed for long video understanding with MLLMs. While the individual components are not entirely new, the specific way they are combined and adapted to the long video context is a contribution. The bin-wise sampling strategy is a nice touch. The tailored reasoning scores for global and local questions is also a meaningful adaptation. However, repeated sampling for improved performance is inspired by previous work on LLM inference, raising concerns about incremental novelty.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a key challenge in the field: how to effectively process long videos with limited input capacity of MLLMs. The results demonstrate consistent improvements across several benchmarks and models, suggesting the effectiveness of the proposed approach.  The scalability of the visual context and self-reward technique can have a strong impact on long video applications. The improvements are substantial and contribute meaningfully to the field's ability to address the long video understanding problem. This contribution facilitates more accurate understanding and reasoning for MLLMs in long video analysis.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-designed experiments and ablations that support the claims.
    *   Demonstrated improvements across multiple datasets and models, showing robustness.
    *   The idea is conceptually simple and easy to implement.
    *   The approach does not require retraining of the MLLMs, making it computationally efficient.
    *   The performance boost using a self-reward mechanism after multiple inferences demonstrates it as a viable strategy to handle the complexity in long videos.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The core idea of repeated sampling with a selection mechanism is inspired by similar strategies in LLMs; the adaptation to video is somewhat straightforward, which might diminish novelty.
    *   The components of the self-reward score (frequency, confidence, reasoning) are relatively standard, and the linear combination might be too simple.
    *   The improvement on VideoLlama2 is not as significant as the other two models, which raises questions about the generalizability of the approach.
    *   While the paper is quite complete, the improvements seem very specific to a specific problem setting (long videos with MLLMs).
    *   There can be a scope of improvement with more sophisticated techniques for weighting the self-reward scores than a simple linear combination.
*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper could inspire more research on visual context sampling strategies for long video understanding.
    *   The self-reward mechanism could be further refined and applied to other video understanding tasks.
    *   The code and models released by the authors could be valuable resources for the community.
    *   The analysis of diverse datasets in long video space makes it a useful resource.

**Justification for the Score:**

While the paper effectively addresses the long video understanding challenge and demonstrates strong empirical results, the novelty is incremental and the improvements are heavily dependent on problem settings. Therefore, the potential influence of the work on the field may be limited, and overall, the paper deserves a good, but not outstanding score.

### Dissecting and Mitigating Diffusion Bias via Mechanistic Interpretability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20483v1)
- **Authors**: Yingdong Shi, Changming Li, Yifan Wang, Yongxiang Zhao, Anqi Pang, Sibei Yang, Jingyi Yu, Kan Ren
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have demonstrated impressive capabilities in synthesizing diverse content. However, despite their high-quality outputs, these models often perpetuate social biases, including those related to gender and race. These biases can potentially contribute to harmful real-world consequences, reinforcing stereotypes and exacerbating inequalities in various social contexts. While existing research on diffusion bias mitigation has predominantly focused on guiding content generation, it often neglects the intrinsic mechanisms within diffusion models that causally drive biased outputs. In this paper, we investigate the internal processes of diffusion models, identifying specific decision-making mechanisms, termed bias features, embedded within the model architecture. By directly manipulating these features, our method precisely isolates and adjusts the elements responsible for bias generation, permitting granular control over the bias levels in the generated content. Through experiments on both unconditional and conditional diffusion models across various social bias attributes, we demonstrate our method's efficacy in managing generation distribution while preserving image quality. We also dissect the discovered model mechanism, revealing different intrinsic features controlling fine-grained aspects of generation, boosting further research on mechanistic interpretability of diffusion models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Dissecting and Mitigating Diffusion Bias via Mechanistic Interpretability":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DIFFLENS, a framework designed to investigate and mitigate social biases within diffusion models.  Unlike existing bias mitigation approaches that focus on guiding content generation or retraining models, DIFFLENS delves into the internal mechanisms of diffusion models to identify specific "bias features" – neuron activities that contribute to biased outputs related to gender, race, and age.  DIFFLENS employs a sparse autoencoder to disentangle neuron activations into a sparse semantic space and then uses a gradient-based approach to pinpoint bias-specific features. By manipulating these identified features, the method allows for fine-grained control over bias levels in generated content while aiming to preserve image quality and other semantic attributes.  The paper presents experimental results on both unconditional and conditional diffusion models, demonstrating DIFFLENS's effectiveness compared to several baselines.  The authors also offer insights into the characteristics of these bias features.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its application of mechanistic interpretability techniques to understand and mitigate bias in diffusion models.  While previous works have addressed bias, they largely focused on input manipulation or retraining.  DIFFLENS offers a different perspective by examining the internal neuron activations and identifying causal relationships between specific features and biased outputs. The combination of sparse autoencoders for disentanglement and gradient-based attribution for feature selection is also a novel technical contribution in this context.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a highly relevant and significant problem: the perpetuation and amplification of social biases by powerful generative models.  The potential impact of this work is substantial. By providing a more interpretable and controllable approach to bias mitigation, DIFFLENS could contribute to the development of fairer and more responsible AI systems. The insights into specific bias features could also inform the design of future diffusion model architectures.

**Strengths:**

*   **Mechanistic Interpretability:** The core strength is the shift towards understanding the *why* behind biased outputs rather than simply trying to correct them.
*   **Fine-grained Control:** DIFFLENS offers a level of control over bias that is potentially more precise than methods that operate at the input prompt or latent space level.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a range of qualitative and quantitative evaluations, comparing against multiple baselines across different attributes and models.
*   **Insights into Bias Features:** The paper sheds light on the characteristics of bias features, which can guide future research on bias mitigation and model design.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity and Implementation:** DIFFLENS involves several steps (sparse autoencoder training, gradient-based attribution, feature manipulation) that might be complex to implement and tune in practice.
*   **Dataset Dependency:** The process of identifying bias features still relies on datasets that contain examples of biased representations.  The paper needs a discussion on how the method perform if the dataset for identifying bias features are carefully curated.
*   **Ethics discussion:** While the paper mentions the need for ethical considerations, a deeper discussion on the limitations of the approach in addressing subtle or intersectional biases would strengthen the paper. Also, an extended discussion about unintended consequences of debiasing one attribute on another is needed.
*   **Limitations of scope:** The approach treats debiased attributes as discrete categories, overlooking more complex individuals.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a significant step forward in addressing bias within diffusion models by focusing on mechanistic interpretability. This approach offers both a more granular control and a deeper understanding of the factors contributing to biased generation, and the experiments clearly validate its effectiveness compared to standard techniques. However, the complexity of implementation and potential dependency on datasets pose limitations. Furthermore, there are gaps for addressing certain racial biases.

**Score: 7.5**

The score reflects the paper's strong novelty and potential impact, offset by practical limitations and the need for further refinement in addressing complex social biases. The insights gleaned from the mechanistic approach provide a valuable contribution, but future work needs to build upon this foundation to create more robust and ethically sound bias mitigation strategies for diffusion models.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Contrastive Learning Guided Latent Diffusion Model for Image-to-Image Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20484v1)
- **Authors**: Qi Si, Bo Wang, Zhao Zhang
- **Abstract**: The diffusion model has demonstrated superior performance in synthesizing diverse and high-quality images for text-guided image translation. However, there remains room for improvement in both the formulation of text prompts and the preservation of reference image content. First, variations in target text prompts can significantly influence the quality of the generated images, and it is often challenging for users to craft an optimal prompt that fully captures the content of the input image. Second, while existing models can introduce desired modifications to specific regions of the reference image, they frequently induce unintended alterations in areas that should remain unchanged. To address these challenges, we propose pix2pix-zeroCon, a zero-shot diffusion-based method that eliminates the need for additional training by leveraging patch-wise contrastive loss. Specifically, we automatically determine the editing direction in the text embedding space based on the reference image and target prompts. Furthermore, to ensure precise content and structural preservation in the edited image, we introduce cross-attention guiding loss and patch-wise contrastive loss between the generated and original image embeddings within a pre-trained diffusion model. Notably, our approach requires no additional training and operates directly on a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method surpasses existing models in image-to-image translation, achieving enhanced fidelity and controllability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes "pix2pix-zeroCon," a novel training-free diffusion model for image-to-image translation. It addresses two key limitations of existing methods: the sensitivity to target text prompts and the imperfect preservation of reference image content.  pix2pix-zeroCon leverages patch-wise contrastive loss and cross-attention guiding loss within a pre-trained diffusion model, eliminating the need for additional training. The method automatically determines the editing direction in the text embedding space and incorporates contrastive learning to better retain structural integrity and content details during translation. Experiments demonstrate improved fidelity and controllability compared to existing image-to-image translation methods. Key contributions include a content structure preservation loss using CUT loss inspired by contrastive learning, and an effective editing direction strategy involving automatic target prompt generation using BLIP and CLIP models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper combines existing techniques in a novel way, primarily by integrating CUT loss into the p2p-zero framework. Automatic target prompt generation using BLIP/CLIP to reduce user burden is also a valuable addition. However, the core components (diffusion models, p2p-zero, contrastive learning) are well-established. The novelty lies in the specific combination and refinement to the image to image translation problem.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its ability to improve image-to-image translation without requiring additional training. Training-free methods are highly desirable because they offer flexibility and accessibility without the need for extensive computational resources or labeled datasets. The improved preservation of image content and structure is a tangible benefit over existing zero-shot methods. The reduction in user effort through automated prompt generation is also a valuable contribution, making the method more user-friendly. Potential areas of improvement are adding more complex datasets where structural integrity of image is more essential.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Training-free approach:** A key advantage, enhancing accessibility and reducing computational cost.
    *   **Improved content preservation:** Effectively addresses a major limitation of existing zero-shot methods.
    *   **Automated prompt generation:** Reduces user burden and improves usability.
    *   **Clear experimental results:** Demonstrates improvements over baseline methods through both qualitative and quantitative evaluations.
    *   **Well-written and organized:**  The paper is generally easy to follow and clearly explains the method and experiments.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental novelty:** The core components are not entirely new, and the approach builds upon existing work like p2p-zero.
    *   **Limited analysis of failure cases:** While the paper shows successful examples, a more in-depth analysis of failure cases or limitations would be beneficial.  The discussion of limitations at the end is a good starting point but could be expanded. The issue of target removal/addition is explicitly identified, pointing to an area for future improvement.
    *   **Computational cost:** While training is avoided, the inference process still involves running a diffusion model, which can be computationally expensive. This aspect could be further discussed.
    *   **Dependency on BLIP/CLIP:**  Performance is inherently tied to the quality of the BLIP and CLIP models used for prompt generation and embedding, respectively. Changes or improvements in these foundational models could impact the results.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in image-to-image translation, particularly in the development of more efficient and user-friendly zero-shot methods. The combination of contrastive learning and attention guidance could inspire new approaches for preserving image content and structure. The automated prompt generation strategy could be adopted or refined by other researchers.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the combination of existing techniques to achieve tangible improvements in zero-shot image to image translation, and considering the limitations in experimental setup, the paper makes a solid contribution. However, the method has certain limitations. Therefore, a score between 6 and 7 appears appropriate.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Underwater Image Enhancement by Convolutional Spiking Neural Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20485v1)
- **Authors**: Vidya Sudevan, Fakhreddine Zayer, Rizwana Kausar, Sajid Javed, Hamad Karki, Giulia De Masi, Jorge Dias
- **Abstract**: Underwater image enhancement (UIE) is fundamental for marine applications, including autonomous vision-based navigation. Deep learning methods using convolutional neural networks (CNN) and vision transformers advanced UIE performance. Recently, spiking neural networks (SNN) have gained attention for their lightweight design, energy efficiency, and scalability. This paper introduces UIE-SNN, the first SNN-based UIE algorithm to improve visibility of underwater images. UIE-SNN is a 19- layered convolutional spiking encoder-decoder framework with skip connections, directly trained using surrogate gradient-based backpropagation through time (BPTT) strategy. We explore and validate the influence of training datasets on energy reduction, a unique advantage of UIE-SNN architecture, in contrast to the conventional learning-based architectures, where energy consumption is model-dependent. UIE-SNN optimizes the loss function in latent space representation to reconstruct clear underwater images. Our algorithm performs on par with its non-spiking counterpart methods in terms of PSNR and structural similarity index (SSIM) at reduced timesteps ($T=5$) and energy consumption of $85\%$. The algorithm is trained on two publicly available benchmark datasets, UIEB and EUVP, and tested on unseen images from UIEB, EUVP, LSUI, U45, and our custom UIE dataset. The UIE-SNN algorithm achieves PSNR of \(17.7801~dB\) and SSIM of \(0.7454\) on UIEB, and PSNR of \(23.1725~dB\) and SSIM of \(0.7890\) on EUVP. UIE-SNN achieves this algorithmic performance with fewer operators (\(147.49\) GSOPs) and energy (\(0.1327~J\)) compared to its non-spiking counterpart (GFLOPs = \(218.88\) and Energy=\(1.0068~J\)). Compared with existing SOTA UIE methods, UIE-SNN achieves an average of \(6.5\times\) improvement in energy efficiency. The source code is available at \href{https://github.com/vidya-rejul/UIE-SNN.git}{UIE-SNN}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UIE-SNN, a novel underwater image enhancement (UIE) algorithm based on a convolutional spiking neural network (SNN). UIE-SNN utilizes a 19-layer encoder-decoder architecture with skip connections and is trained directly using surrogate gradient-based backpropagation through time (BPTT). A key focus is on energy efficiency, a known advantage of SNNs.  The authors evaluate the performance of UIE-SNN against its non-spiking (CNN) counterpart and analyze the impact of training datasets and hyperparameters (threshold and timesteps) on both image quality and energy consumption. Results indicate that UIE-SNN achieves comparable or slightly lower image quality (PSNR, SSIM) with significantly reduced energy consumption and computational complexity. The code is made available to reproduce the results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in being the **first** SNN-based approach for UIE.  While SNNs have been applied in other computer vision tasks, this is a novel application. The direct training of an SNN for UIE, optimizing membrane potentials using BPTT with surrogate gradients, is also a key contribution. Moreover, the study provides experimental evidence supporting the effectiveness of such a direct approach.
    The hyperparameter analysis specifically tailored to UIE with SNNs provides valuable insights for further research. The examination of the influence of the training set on the energy consumption is also a plus, as it unveils the SNN’s potential to learn task-dependent features.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the growing need for energy-efficient algorithms in resource-constrained environments, such as underwater robotics. Deep learning approaches, while powerful, are often computationally expensive. By demonstrating the feasibility of using SNNs for UIE with competitive performance, the paper opens the door for more sustainable UIE solutions.
    While UIE-SNN achieves excellent results, it is important to note that the proposed UIE-SNN framework has limitations and faces certain challenges. In particular, it is important to further investigate the framework's efficiency when exposed to several diverse image degradation problems simultaneously.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear presentation of the algorithm and experimental setup.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation against a CNN baseline and cross-validation on diverse datasets.
    *   Detailed analysis of hyperparameters and their impact on performance and energy consumption.
    *   Availability of code to allow the reproducibility of the results.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper does not compare its results against other existing SNN-based methods, limiting the assessment of UIE-SNN performance relative to state-of-the-art SNNs, and it only compares with conventional methods.
    *   Ablation of more architectural aspects of the SNN is not performed.
    *   The performance metrics are comparable to the CNN-based ones, but not consistently superior. This limits the significance.
    *   While the discussion of energy consumption is detailed, further examination of specialised neuromorphic hardware implementation and scalability is needed.
*   **Impact:** This paper's impact is potentially significant but depends on future research building upon this foundation. It establishes a viable starting point for SNN-based UIE. Future work could explore more advanced SNN architectures or hybrid SNN-CNN approaches to further improve image quality and energy efficiency. The study provides practical knowledge that contributes towards a more energy-efficient implementation of UIE systems.

*   **Rationale:** The novelty is reasonably high (first SNN UIE), the significance is there, and the experimental study is of good quality. The main weakness is that the actual performance increase (if any) is small. Further experiments would strengthen the claims.

### VPO: Aligning Text-to-Video Generation Models with Prompt Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20491v1)
- **Authors**: Jiale Cheng, Ruiliang Lyu, Xiaotao Gu, Xiao Liu, Jiazheng Xu, Yida Lu, Jiayan Teng, Zhuoyi Yang, Yuxiao Dong, Jie Tang, Hongning Wang, Minlie Huang
- **Abstract**: Video generation models have achieved remarkable progress in text-to-video tasks. These models are typically trained on text-video pairs with highly detailed and carefully crafted descriptions, while real-world user inputs during inference are often concise, vague, or poorly structured. This gap makes prompt optimization crucial for generating high-quality videos. Current methods often rely on large language models (LLMs) to refine prompts through in-context learning, but suffer from several limitations: they may distort user intent, omit critical details, or introduce safety risks. Moreover, they optimize prompts without considering the impact on the final video quality, which can lead to suboptimal results. To address these issues, we introduce VPO, a principled framework that optimizes prompts based on three core principles: harmlessness, accuracy, and helpfulness. The generated prompts faithfully preserve user intents and, more importantly, enhance the safety and quality of generated videos. To achieve this, VPO employs a two-stage optimization approach. First, we construct and refine a supervised fine-tuning (SFT) dataset based on principles of safety and alignment. Second, we introduce both text-level and video-level feedback to further optimize the SFT model with preference learning. Our extensive experiments demonstrate that VPO significantly improves safety, alignment, and video quality compared to baseline methods. Moreover, VPO shows strong generalization across video generation models. Furthermore, we demonstrate that VPO could outperform and be combined with RLHF methods on video generation models, underscoring the effectiveness of VPO in aligning video generation models. Our code and data are publicly available at https://github.com/thu-coai/VPO.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and rigorous critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VPO, a framework for aligning text-to-video generation models with user intent and safety principles. It addresses the gap between carefully crafted training data and real-world, often vague or unsafe user inputs. VPO employs a two-stage approach: (1) principle-based supervised fine-tuning (SFT) to create a prompt refinement model adhering to harmlessness, accuracy, and helpfulness; and (2) multi-feedback preference optimization, using both text-level and video-level feedback to further refine the SFT model. Experiments demonstrate VPO's effectiveness in improving safety, alignment, and video quality compared to existing methods. The framework also shows strong generalization across different video generation models and can be combined with RLHF techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its principled approach to prompt optimization for text-to-video generation.  Prior work often relies solely on LLMs for prompt refinement without explicitly addressing safety concerns, user intent alignment, and the impact on final video quality. VPO's explicit focus on *harmlessness, accuracy, and helpfulness*, implemented through a two-stage refinement process, presents a clear advancement. The integration of both text-level and video-level feedback for preference optimization is also a valuable contribution, addressing the limitations of text-only prompt optimization methods. The way VPO is designed as a "prompt-aligned" method and its ability to be used in RLHF is also an interesting novelty.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in the field of text-to-video generation: the discrepancy between training data and real-world user inputs. By improving the safety, alignment, and quality of generated videos, VPO has the potential to make these models more accessible, reliable, and less prone to generating harmful content. Demonstrating generalizability across different video generation models further enhances its practical impact. The success of VPO in improving video quality, as measured by standard benchmarks and human evaluation, reinforces its significance.

* **Strengths:**
    *   The principle-driven approach provides a clear and well-defined framework.
    *   The two-stage refinement process effectively addresses different aspects of prompt optimization (safety, intent alignment, video quality).
    *   Extensive experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of VPO compared to baselines.
    *   Generalization across different video generation models is shown.
    *   The combination with RLHF methods is a significant finding.
    *   Well-written and clearly presented.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the principles of harmlessness, accuracy, and helpfulness are well-established in LLMs, the paper does not explore the potential biases or limitations of these principles in the specific context of video generation.  A more critical discussion of these ethical considerations would strengthen the paper.
    *   The experiments, while comprehensive, are limited to CogVideoX and Open-Sora. Further evaluation on a wider range of models, especially those with different architectures or training datasets, would increase the robustness of the findings.
    *   The dependency on LLMs for data creation and prompt refinement introduces a potential for bias in the resulting prompts. The methods employed to mitigate this bias, while discussed, could be elaborated. The sensitivity of VPO on a poor choice of the LLM that creates the data is important to be explored.

*   **Potential Influence:** VPO has the potential to become a standard technique for aligning text-to-video generation models. The principle-driven framework can inspire further research into more sophisticated prompt optimization strategies. The demonstrated combination with RLHF techniques opens up new avenues for improving video generation models.

**Justification:**

While the core principles are adapted from LLM alignment, the *specific application* of these principles to *video prompt optimization*, the design of the two-stage refinement framework, and the demonstrated empirical results justify a high score. However, the weaknesses mentioned above prevent it from being considered truly exceptional.

### MLLM-Selector: Necessity and Diversity-driven High-Value Data Selection for Enhanced Visual Instruction Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20502v1)
- **Authors**: Yiwei Ma, Guohai Xu, Xiaoshuai Sun, Jiayi Ji, Jie Lou, Debing Zhang, Rongrong Ji
- **Abstract**: Visual instruction tuning (VIT) has emerged as a crucial technique for enabling multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) to follow user instructions adeptly. Yet, a significant gap persists in understanding the attributes of high-quality instruction tuning data and frameworks for its automated selection. To address this, we introduce MLLM-Selector, an automated approach that identifies valuable data for VIT by weighing necessity and diversity. Our process starts by randomly sampling a subset from the VIT data pool to fine-tune a pretrained model, thus creating a seed model with an initial ability to follow instructions. Then, leveraging the seed model, we calculate necessity scores for each sample in the VIT data pool to identify samples pivotal for enhancing model performance. Our findings underscore the importance of mixing necessity and diversity in data choice, leading to the creation of MLLM-Selector, our methodology that fuses necessity scoring with strategic sampling for superior data refinement. Empirical results indicate that within identical experimental conditions, MLLM-Selector surpasses LLaVA-1.5 in some benchmarks with less than 1% of the data and consistently exceeds performance across all validated benchmarks when using less than 50%.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces MLLM-Selector, a novel method for automatically selecting high-quality data for Visual Instruction Tuning (VIT) by balancing necessity and diversity. The approach leverages necessity scores derived from a seed model and a grouped sampling strategy to ensure a comprehensive and effective dataset for training multi-modal large language models (MLLMs).  Experiments demonstrate that MLLM-Selector surpasses baselines like LLaVA-1.5, achieving superior performance with less data on some benchmarks and maintaining competitiveness across all validated benchmarks.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a practical and important problem: the quality of visual instruction tuning data and the lack of automated methods for its selection.  The concept of balancing "necessity" and "diversity" is intuitive and potentially effective. Deriving necessity scores from a seed model is also a reasonable approach. The strength of the paper lies in the experimental results, which demonstrate performance improvements over LLaVA-1.5 using smaller datasets and showing competitive performance even with equivalent dataset sizes.

However, several aspects require critical scrutiny:

1.  **Novelty:** While the combination of necessity and diversity is well-motivated, the specific implementation using a seed model and grouped sampling is not radically novel. Data selection techniques have been explored extensively in other domains of machine learning, and it's essential to see a clear distinction beyond adapting existing ideas. The main contribution, therefore, hinges on the specifics of how necessity scores are calculated and integrated with the grouped sampling strategy within the VIT context. The "necessity" score sounds like a confidence score of prediction based on the seed model, which is not very innovative.
2.  **Significance:** The reported performance improvements, while encouraging, need more in-depth analysis.  The paper demonstrates better performance in some benchmarks. The actual magnitude of the performance gains, especially the gains by just using better selected data (and not just the amount of data) should be significant. Moreover, does MLLM-Selector yield a consistently better model on downstream tasks than the manual dataset construction? A more extensive analysis across a wider range of downstream tasks and benchmarks is needed to establish the true significance.
3.  **Generalizability:** The experiments are conducted using LLaVA-1.5 and Vicuna.  It's unclear how well MLLM-Selector would generalize to other MLLM architectures, different data distributions, or tasks beyond those explicitly evaluated.  The hyperparameters, like group size k and the seed data set ratio, also need more evaluation and analysis for generalizability. The analysis of how sensitive performance is to the ratio of "necessity" and "diversity" should be better detailed, rather than just provide several numbers.
4.  **Clarity of Methodological Details:** The description of the necessity score calculation and the implementation details of the grouped sampling strategy could be more precise.  For example, how is `pe` calculated efficiently, and what specific diversity metrics (beyond grouping) are considered?
5.  **Comparison with existing methods:** The paper mentions some existing data selection methods, such as InstructionGPT-4 and SELE-FILTER, but the comparison with them isn't detailed. More explicit comparion and contrast should be performed.

In summary, the paper presents a practically relevant data selection method for VIT and demonstrates some performance gains. However, it needs further validation across diverse settings and architectures and a more detailed comparison with existing data selection techniques.

### Vision-Amplified Semantic Entropy for Hallucination Detection in Medical Visual Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20504v1)
- **Authors**: Zehui Liao, Shishuai Hu, Ke Zou, Huazhu Fu, Liangli Zhen, Yong Xia
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have demonstrated significant potential in medical Visual Question Answering (VQA). Yet, they remain prone to hallucinations-incorrect responses that contradict input images, posing substantial risks in clinical decision-making. Detecting these hallucinations is essential for establishing trust in MLLMs among clinicians and patients, thereby enabling their real-world adoption. Current hallucination detection methods, especially semantic entropy (SE), have demonstrated promising hallucination detection capacity for LLMs. However, adapting SE to medical MLLMs by incorporating visual perturbations presents a dilemma. Weak perturbations preserve image content and ensure clinical validity, but may be overlooked by medical MLLMs, which tend to over rely on language priors. In contrast, strong perturbations can distort essential diagnostic features, compromising clinical interpretation. To address this issue, we propose Vision Amplified Semantic Entropy (VASE), which incorporates weak image transformations and amplifies the impact of visual input, to improve hallucination detection in medical VQA. We first estimate the semantic predictive distribution under weak visual transformations to preserve clinical validity, and then amplify visual influence by contrasting this distribution with that derived from a distorted image. The entropy of the resulting distribution is estimated as VASE. Experiments on two medical open-ended VQA datasets demonstrate that VASE consistently outperforms existing hallucination detection methods.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper based on the provided OCR output.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Vision-Amplified Semantic Entropy (VASE), a new approach for detecting hallucinations in medical Visual Question Answering (VQA) systems powered by multimodal large language models (MLLMs). VASE aims to improve hallucination detection by incorporating weak image transformations and amplifying the influence of visual input. The method involves estimating the semantic probability distribution of responses under weak visual transformations, then contrasting this distribution with one derived from a distorted image.  The entropy of the resulting distribution (VASE) is used to identify potential hallucinations. Experiments on two medical VQA datasets (MIMIC-Diff-VQA and VQA-RAD) demonstrate that VASE outperforms existing hallucination detection methods. The paper investigates the contributions of visual transformations and contrasting, finding that the method benefits from both components.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the specific combination of techniques for addressing hallucinations in medical VQA. While semantic entropy for hallucination detection in LLMs is not entirely novel (as cited by Farquhar et al.), the *adaptation* to medical imaging, the use of *weak* image transformations (specifically chosen to preserve clinical validity), and the *amplification* of visual influence using a contrastive approach are new contributions. Existing semantic entropy based techniques can be insufficient when applied to medical images because MLLMs tend to over rely on language priors, rendering them insensitive to visual inputs. The contrasting strategy amplifies sensitivity to visual input. This problem is specifically addressed by VASE's novel combination of the three elements. While there is a clear rationale behind VASE, it still seems more like a novel assembly of proven components.

*   **Significance:** Hallucinations in medical VQA are a serious concern because they can lead to misdiagnoses and incorrect treatment plans. Therefore, any improvement in hallucination detection is significant. The results presented, which indicate that VASE consistently outperforms other methods on two different datasets, support the claim that VASE is a promising approach. The work specifically addresses the limitations of current methods in the context of medical imaging, which is important because medical images have unique characteristics. The ablation study demonstrates the importance of each element of the proposed framework. If the method can be validated in further tests, this might be important in ensuring that medical professionals trust the MLLMs and use them in real world scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined methodology with a clear explanation of each step.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on two relevant datasets.
    *   Ablation studies to understand the contribution of each component.
    *   Comparison to several existing methods.
    *   The chosen weak transformations aimed at preserving clinical validity are a notable consideration.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The individual components (semantic entropy, image transformations, contrastive approaches) are not entirely novel; the novelty rests in the specific combination. While this combination is well motivated, a larger leap in innovation would have strengthened the significance.
    *   The performance gains, while consistent, are not dramatic. The difference between VASE and SE in terms of AUC is less than 2% in multiple configurations which isn't necessarily highly meaningful on its own.

*   **Potential Impact:** The potential impact is reasonably high if the method proves to be robust and generalizable to other medical imaging modalities and VQA tasks. Improving the reliability of medical VQA systems could have a significant positive impact on patient care. Further evaluation on a wider range of datasets is needed to fully assess its generalizability.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the clear problem definition, the novel combination of techniques, the comprehensive experimental evaluation, and consistent improvements over existing methods, this paper makes a solid contribution. However, the incremental nature of the improvement over existing methods, the absence of a breakthrough innovation, and the limitation to two datasets prevent it from receiving a higher score. The method could also have been improved with some theoretical analysis or proofs, but this is missing.

### Explainable ICD Coding via Entity Linking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20508v1)
- **Authors**: Leonor Barreiros, Isabel Coutinho, Gonçalo M. Correia, Bruno Martins
- **Abstract**: Clinical coding is a critical task in healthcare, although traditional methods for automating clinical coding may not provide sufficient explicit evidence for coders in production environments. This evidence is crucial, as medical coders have to make sure there exists at least one explicit passage in the input health record that justifies the attribution of a code. We therefore propose to reframe the task as an entity linking problem, in which each document is annotated with its set of codes and respective textual evidence, enabling better human-machine collaboration. By leveraging parameter-efficient fine-tuning of Large Language Models (LLMs), together with constrained decoding, we introduce three approaches to solve this problem that prove effective at disambiguating clinical mentions and that perform well in few-shot scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Explainable ICD Coding via Entity Linking":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitations of traditional Multi-Label Classification (MLC) approaches to ICD coding, particularly their lack of explainability.  It proposes reframing the task as an entity linking problem where medical records are annotated with ICD-10 codes and the corresponding textual evidence (mentions) within the record.  The authors explore three approaches based on the BioMistral 7B Large Language Model (LLM): in-context learning (ICL), supervised fine-tuning (SFT), and a GENRE-inspired model (INSGENEL) which predicts multiple mention-entity pairs in a single pass. They evaluate these approaches on public datasets, comparing performance in terms of accuracy and also conduct a few-shot analysis to assess how well the models generalize to unseen codes. The results show that fine-tuned models outperform the ICL approach, and the proposed approach has potential in scenarios with few instances.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of framing ICD coding as an entity linking task to improve explainability is not entirely novel. However, the paper's contribution lies in its systematic exploration of this framing with recent advances in LLMs (specifically BioMistral), applying constrained decoding, and comparing different fine-tuning strategies.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical issue in clinical coding: the need for explainable AI. The ability to trace a code back to specific textual evidence in the medical record is essential for trust and adoption by medical professionals. The paper's demonstration that LLMs can effectively perform this task, even in few-shot settings, has practical implications for developing more reliable and transparent automated coding systems.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of traditional MLC and the need for explainability.
    *   **Systematic approach:** The authors explore several approaches to the entity linking task using a unified framework.
    *   **Empirical evaluation:** The experiments are well-designed and provide valuable insights into the performance of different models, including analysis of partial results.
    *   **Practical considerations:** The discussion of practical highlights, such as the benefits of INSGENEL-BIOMISTRAL for production scenarios, adds to the paper's value.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Datasets:** The reliance on publicly available datasets, which represent only a limited range of clinical data, may limit the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Gold Mentions:** The experiments use gold mentions (pre-detected mentions), which simplifies the task. A true end-to-end system would need to incorporate mention detection, which adds complexity.
    *   **Limited comparison to state-of-the-art systems:** While the authors do compare against existing results for CodiEsp, the comparison is difficult due to the use of gold mentions. Also, the discussed systems are not the current state-of-the-art. The paper could have benefitted from more up-to-date benchmark models.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more explainable and trustworthy automated ICD coding systems. By demonstrating the effectiveness of LLMs for entity linking in this domain, the authors encourage further research and development in this area. However, to realize significant impact, future work needs to address the limitations outlined above, including handling mention detection and working with more representative datasets.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the above, the paper makes a useful contribution to the field by exploring the entity linking framework for explainable ICD coding using modern LLMs. The systematic comparison of different approaches and the practical insights gained from the experiments are valuable. However, the reliance on gold mentions, the limited datasets, the lack of comprehensive comparison and the need to address the mention detection task hinder its impact.

### MAR-3D: Progressive Masked Auto-regressor for High-Resolution 3D Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20519v1)
- **Authors**: Jinnan Chen, Lingting Zhu, Zeyu Hu, Shengju Qian, Yugang Chen, Xin Wang, Gim Hee Lee
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in auto-regressive transformers have revolutionized generative modeling across different domains, from language processing to visual generation, demonstrating remarkable capabilities. However, applying these advances to 3D generation presents three key challenges: the unordered nature of 3D data conflicts with sequential next-token prediction paradigm, conventional vector quantization approaches incur substantial compression loss when applied to 3D meshes, and the lack of efficient scaling strategies for higher resolution latent prediction. To address these challenges, we introduce MAR-3D, which integrates a pyramid variational autoencoder with a cascaded masked auto-regressive transformer (Cascaded MAR) for progressive latent upscaling in the continuous space. Our architecture employs random masking during training and auto-regressive denoising in random order during inference, naturally accommodating the unordered property of 3D latent tokens. Additionally, we propose a cascaded training strategy with condition augmentation that enables efficiently up-scale the latent token resolution with fast convergence. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MAR-3D not only achieves superior performance and generalization capabilities compared to existing methods but also exhibits enhanced scaling capabilities compared to joint distribution modeling approaches (e.g., diffusion transformers).
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize and critically evaluate the provided paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MAR-3D, a novel framework for high-resolution 3D mesh generation. It addresses key challenges in applying auto-regressive transformers to 3D data, specifically the unordered nature of 3D data, compression loss from vector quantization, and the lack of efficient scaling strategies. MAR-3D uses a pyramid variational autoencoder (VAE) to capture multi-scale geometric information and a cascaded masked auto-regressive transformer (Cascaded MAR) for progressive latent upscaling in continuous space.  The architecture incorporates random masking during training and auto-regressive denoising in random order during inference to handle the unordered nature of 3D latent tokens. A cascaded training strategy with condition augmentation enables efficient latent token resolution upscaling with fast convergence. Experiments demonstrate that MAR-3D achieves superior performance and generalization compared to existing methods and enhanced scaling capabilities compared to joint distribution modeling approaches (e.g., diffusion transformers).

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a solid combination of existing techniques with some modifications tailored for 3D mesh generation. The pyramid VAE and cascaded auto-regressive transformer are not entirely novel on their own, but their integration, specifically addressing the challenges of 3D data, is a meaningful contribution. The random masking for unordered data and condition augmentation are clever adaptations that improve performance. The idea of progressive generation to address the computational cost of transformer architecture while maintaining detail is also significant. The use of both VAE and auto-regressive modeling helps to address the drawbacks of both, yielding a more powerful model overall.

**Significance:** The significance of this work lies in its ability to generate high-resolution, detailed 3D meshes, a task with numerous applications in gaming, AR/VR, and other fields. The reported improvements in performance and generalization capabilities, relative to existing methods, suggest a tangible advance in the state-of-the-art. The scaling strategy contributes a meaningful direction towards creating larger models and data structures for generative modeling. Furthermore, the focus on the continuous latent space circumvents the problems of using quantization in the latent space, improving realism.

**Strengths:**

*   **Effective Combination of Techniques:** The combination of a pyramid VAE and cascaded auto-regressive transformer is well-motivated and demonstrably effective.
*   **Addressing 3D-Specific Challenges:** The paper directly addresses the challenges associated with applying auto-regressive models to 3D data, such as the unordered nature of 3D data and efficient scaling strategies.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The quantitative and qualitative results demonstrate the superiority of MAR-3D over existing methods.
*   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the integration of techniques is well-done, the individual components are not groundbreaking. The novelty is in the specific adaptation to the 3D domain and the combination of these techniques.
*   **Computational Cost:**  Although they present better results, the computational cost of the auto-regressive generation and VAE model has to be justified. While it can yield faster results, the cost of training might not be justified for smaller datasets.
*   **Reliance on Supervised Data:** The framework still relies heavily on supervised data for training, which may limit its applicability to domains with limited labeled data.

**Potential Influence:** The work is likely to influence future research in 3D generation, particularly in the development of more efficient and scalable methods. The cascaded generation strategy and the adaptation of auto-regressive models to 3D data are valuable contributions that could inspire further innovation.

**Justification for Score:**
Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I would assign a score of **7**.  The paper is a solid contribution with well-supported claims and tangible improvements over existing methods. While the novelty is not revolutionary, the specific adaptation of techniques to address challenges in 3D mesh generation is a worthwhile contribution. The improvements in resolution and level of detail are significant and could enable new applications, but it comes at the cost of computational resources, which might make it inaccessible for some people.
