* **Potential Influence:** Free4D has the potential to influence future research in 4D scene generation by demonstrating the feasibility of a tuning-free approach and by providing a modular pipeline that can be further improved with advancements in underlying technologies like diffusion models and dynamic reconstruction. The emphasis on spatial-temporal consistency and scene-level generation is also a positive direction. However, the impact may be limited if the resulting realism is not significantly improved.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 6/10. While Free4D presents a novel and potentially significant approach to 4D scene generation, it has notable limitations. The tuning-free aspect and focus on scene-level consistency are valuable contributions. The lack of detail in quantitative comparison, issues regarding limited realism, and reliance on existing model performance limit its potential impact. Further improvements in realism, a more comprehensive evaluation, and public code release will be necessary to truly solidify its significance.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Mobile-MMLU: A Mobile Intelligence Language Understanding Benchmark
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20786v1)
- **Authors**: Sondos Mahmoud Bsharat, Mukul Ranjan, Aidar Myrzakhan, Jiacheng Liu, Bowei Guo, Shengkun Tang, Zhuang Liu, Yuanzhi Li, Zhiqiang Shen
- **Abstract**: Rapid advancements in large language models (LLMs) have increased interest in deploying them on mobile devices for on-device AI applications. Mobile users interact differently with LLMs compared to desktop users, creating unique expectations and data biases. Current benchmark datasets primarily target at server and desktop environments, and there is a notable lack of extensive datasets specifically designed for mobile contexts. Additionally, mobile devices face strict limitations in storage and computing resources, constraining model size and capabilities, thus requiring optimized efficiency and prioritized knowledge. To address these challenges, we introduce Mobile-MMLU, a large-scale benchmark dataset tailored for mobile intelligence. It consists of 16,186 questions across 80 mobile-related fields, designed to evaluate LLM performance in realistic mobile scenarios. A challenging subset, Mobile-MMLU-Pro, provides advanced evaluation similar in size to MMLU-Pro but significantly more difficult than our standard full set. Both benchmarks use multiple-choice, order-invariant questions focused on practical mobile interactions, such as recipe suggestions, travel planning, and essential daily tasks. The dataset emphasizes critical mobile-specific metrics like inference latency, energy consumption, memory usage, and response quality, offering comprehensive insights into model performance under mobile constraints. Moreover, it prioritizes privacy and adaptability, assessing models' ability to perform on-device processing, maintain user privacy, and adapt to personalized usage patterns. Mobile-MMLU family offers a standardized framework for developing and comparing mobile-optimized LLMs, enabling advancements in productivity and decision-making within mobile computing environments. Our code and data are available at: https://github.com/VILA-Lab/Mobile-MMLU.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Mobile-MMLU paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Mobile-MMLU, a new benchmark dataset designed for evaluating the language understanding capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in mobile contexts. Addressing a gap in existing benchmarks, Mobile-MMLU focuses on real-world mobile usage scenarios and constraints, such as limited resources (memory, power) and user interaction patterns. It includes 16,186 questions across 80 mobile-related topics. A subset, Mobile-MMLU-Pro, is created through multi-model consistency filtering to provide a more challenging and consistent evaluation set. The authors emphasize the importance of evaluating models in scenarios that reflect how users actually interact with mobile devices, as well as considering factors like privacy and personalization. The paper details the data creation pipeline, including field selection, question generation (using LLMs and human verification), similarity filtering, and measures to reduce selection bias.  Evaluations with various open-source LLMs are presented, demonstrating the benchmark's ability to discriminate model performance in mobile-relevant tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its creation of a mobile-centric LLM benchmark. Existing benchmarks are mostly server- or desktop-focused. This targeted focus on mobile scenarios, which include tasks that are different from traditional academic benchmarks (e.g., travel planning, first aid), is a valuable contribution. The creation of Mobile-MMLU-Pro as a more challenging subset is also a good step towards robust evaluation. The attention to removing selection biases, particularly concerning answer length, is a plus.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes a significant step towards practical AI on mobile devices. The focus on user-centric and privacy-aware models could lead to more user-friendly AI implementations on-device.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The dataset is large and covers a diverse set of mobile-relevant domains.
    *   The data creation pipeline is well-documented, addressing potential biases.
    *   The inclusion of both a full set and a challenging subset caters to different evaluation needs.
    *   The paper provides empirical evidence demonstrating that Mobile-MMLU reveals different model performance characteristics than traditional benchmarks.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the paper mentions metrics like inference latency, energy consumption, and memory usage, the benchmark itself seems primarily focused on accuracy. It does not include tools or methodologies for measuring these critical mobile-specific metrics. A more holistic evaluation framework would measure all the variables.
    *   The reliance on GPT-4 and other large models for question generation, even with human verification, could introduce unintended biases or limitations reflecting the knowledge of those models. The verification process involves a three-LLM voting scheme, which could also result in bias toward the consensus opinion of those three particular models and not necessarily what's best for mobile use.
    *   The paper only presents zero-shot evaluation results. While valuable, few-shot or fine-tuned results would be more informative in real-world deployment scenarios.
    *   The ethical considerations section is brief. A more in-depth discussion of potential biases in the data and their societal impact would be beneficial.
    *   The "order-invariance" may not be fully justified if certain biases or ordering still affects the LLM's performance.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development and evaluation of mobile-optimized LLMs.  It provides a valuable resource for researchers and developers looking to improve the performance and efficiency of LLMs on mobile devices. The insights gained from this benchmark could lead to more personalized, privacy-aware, and resource-efficient mobile AI applications.

*   **Overall:** The paper addresses a clear need in the LLM evaluation landscape and provides a valuable resource for the mobile AI community. However, several weaknesses, particularly the limited focus on resource-related metrics and the potential for LLM-introduced biases, need to be addressed in future work.

Score: 7

**Justification:**
The paper is useful, novel, and fills a gap, making it clearly above average. However, the reliance on the accuracy metric is not the most suitable aspect of mobile LLM. Furthermore, if the biases, if properly evaluated, show significant problems, it could be much lower. The lack of code provided is problematic. But overall, it deserves to be in the above-average range.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10
